\subsection{Build the physical scene from
numbers}\label{build-the-physical-scene-from-numbers}

\subsubsection{Units}\label{units}

The implementation uses the usual MuJoCo metric quantities, made
explicit by variable and metric names:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Quantity
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Unit
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Example
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
length and position & metre, \texttt{m} & ball radius
\texttt{0.12\ m} \\
mass & kilogram, \texttt{kg} & ball mass \texttt{0.62\ kg} \\
time & second, \texttt{s} & control interval \texttt{0.02\ s} \\
linear velocity & metres per second, \texttt{m/s} & command
\texttt{0.15\ m/s} \\
angle & radian internally & joint offsets and yaw command \\
angular velocity & radians per second, \texttt{rad/s} & yaw and ball
rotation rates \\
reported tilt & degree & evaluator limits tilt to less than
\texttt{50\ deg} \\
force & newton, \texttt{N} & assistance spring and damping forces \\
torque & newton-metre, \texttt{N\ m} & assistance torques \\
\end{longtable}
}

A radian is an angle measured by arc length divided by radius. For
orientation, \texttt{pi\ rad\ =\ 180\ deg}. The policy's 14 action
values are joint-angle offsets in radians, not motor torques.

\subsubsection{Solver, floor, ball, and visual
mesh}\label{solver-floor-ball-and-visual-mesh}

The scene starts from the full-collision Microduck robot and adds a
floor and a free body:

\begin{figure}
\centering
\includegraphics[width=6.2in,height=5.5in,alt={Schematic basketball geometry using the implemented radius and spawn heights}]{assets/diagrams/schematic-basketball-geometry.png}
\caption{Schematic basketball geometry using the implemented radius and
spawn heights}
\end{figure}

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}rlx/\allowbreak{}environm\allowbreak{}ents/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/rlx/environments/basketball.py}
\NormalTok{BALL\_RADIUS }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.12}
\NormalTok{BALL\_MASS }\OperatorTok{=} \FloatTok{0.62}

\NormalTok{spec.option.timestep }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ C.PHYSICS\_DT}
\NormalTok{spec.option.integrator }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mujoco.mjtIntegrator.mjINT\_IMPLICITFAST}
\NormalTok{spec.option.cone }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ mujoco.mjtCone.mjCONE\_PYRAMIDAL}
\NormalTok{spec.option.iterations }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{10}
\NormalTok{spec.option.ls\_iterations }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{20}

\NormalTok{spec.worldbody.add\_geom(}
\NormalTok{    name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"floor"}\NormalTok{, }\BuiltInTok{type}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mujoco.mjtGeom.mjGEOM\_PLANE,}
\NormalTok{    size}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.05}\NormalTok{], material}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"basketball\_ground\_mat"}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{    friction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.005}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.0001}\NormalTok{],}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{ball }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ spec.worldbody.add\_body(}
\NormalTok{    name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"basketball"}\NormalTok{, pos}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, BALL\_RADIUS }\OperatorTok{+} \FloatTok{0.001}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ball.add\_freejoint(name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"basketball\_freejoint"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{ball.add\_geom(}
\NormalTok{    name}\OperatorTok{=}\StringTok{"ball\_sphere"}\NormalTok{, }\BuiltInTok{type}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{mujoco.mjtGeom.mjGEOM\_SPHERE,}
\NormalTok{    size}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[BALL\_RADIUS, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{], mass}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{BALL\_MASS,}
\NormalTok{    friction}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{[}\FloatTok{1.2}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.01}\NormalTok{, }\FloatTok{0.001}\NormalTok{], priority}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, condim}\OperatorTok{=}\DecValTok{4}\NormalTok{,}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The sphere's centre begins at \texttt{z\ =\ 0.121\ m}, one millimetre
above a radius of \texttt{0.12\ m}. Its physical geom has mass and
contact. A second mesh geom supplies the basketball appearance but has
\texttt{mass=0}, \texttt{contype=0}, and \texttt{conaffinity=0}; it
cannot secretly support the robot. The texture and mesh come from
\texttt{microduc\allowbreak{}k-\allowbreak{}playgrou\allowbreak{}nd/\allowbreak{}src/\allowbreak{}mjlab\_\allowbreak{}microduc\allowbreak{}k/\allowbreak{}robot/\allowbreak{}assets/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll/\allowbreak{}}.

The floor uses checkerboard texture colours and friction
\texttt{{[}1,\ 0.005,\ 0.0001{]}}. The ball uses
\texttt{{[}1.2,\ 0.01,\ 0.001{]}}, contact dimension 4, and priority 1.
These are simulator contact parameters, not measured certificates for a
particular real floor or ball.

The free joint gives the basketball seven configuration values: three
position coordinates plus a four-value quaternion. It also gives six
velocity values: three linear and three angular. Tests lock the complete
model at \texttt{nq=28}, \texttt{nv=26}, and \texttt{nu=14}.

\subsubsection{Timing}\label{timing}

The physics step is

\[
\Delta t_{\text{physics}}=0.005\text{ s}.
\]

Each policy action is held for four physics steps:

\[
\Delta t_{\text{control}}
=4(0.005)
=0.02\text{ s},
\]

so the policy runs at

\[
f_{\text{control}}=\frac{1}{0.02}=50\text{ Hz}.
\]

A 60-second evaluation therefore contains exactly

\[
\frac{60}{0.02}=3000
\]

control steps per trial.

\subsubsection{Spawn geometry}\label{spawn-geometry}

The ball centre is at \texttt{0.121\ m}. The robot root is placed at

\[
z_{\text{root}}
=2R+0.128
=2(0.12)+0.128
=0.368\text{ m}.
\]

The root is therefore

\[
0.368-0.121=0.247\text{ m}
\]

above the ball centre and about

\[
0.368-(0.121+0.12)=0.127\text{ m}
\]

above the ball's top. The reset test locks
\texttt{root\_\allowbreak{}above\_\allowbreak{}ball\_\allowbreak{}m} to
\texttt{0.247}.

The robot starts upright near the apex. Its horizontal root position is
sampled within \texttt{+-0.01\ m}, roll and pitch within
\texttt{+-2\ deg}, yaw across \texttt{{[}-pi,\ pi{]}} when random yaw is
enabled, and every joint within \texttt{+-0.05\ rad} of the default
pose. The ball starts at rest. This task does not teach mounting the
ball or recovering from the ground.

\subsection{The exact 61-observation and 14-action
contract}\label{the-exact-61-observation-and-14-action-contract}

\subsubsection{Observation slices}\label{observation-slices}

The actor always receives a \texttt{float32{[}61{]}} vector:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lrrl@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Python slice & Indices & Width & Meaning \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{0:3} & 0-2 & 3 & trunk angular velocity \\
\texttt{3:6} & 3-5 & 3 & gravity projected into the trunk frame \\
\texttt{6:20} & 6-19 & 14 & joint position minus the default pose \\
\texttt{20:34} & 20-33 & 14 & joint velocity, delayed one control
step \\
\texttt{34:48} & 34-47 & 14 & previous raw actor action \\
\texttt{48:51} & 48-50 & 3 & forward, lateral, and yaw velocity
command \\
\texttt{51:55} & 51-54 & 4 & head-command padding, forced to zero \\
\texttt{55:61} & 55-60 & 6 & body-command padding, forced to zero \\
\end{longtable}
}

The assembly is direct:

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{microduc\allowbreak{}k\_\allowbreak{}local/\allowbreak{}src/\allowbreak{}microduc\allowbreak{}k\_\allowbreak{}local/\allowbreak{}walk\_\allowbreak{}env.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# microduck\_local/src/microduck\_local/walk\_env.py}
\NormalTok{obs }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ np.empty(C.OBS\_DIM, np.float32)}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ gyro}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ gravity}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{6}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ joint\_pos}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{34}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ joint\_vel}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{34}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{48}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.last\_action}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{48}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{51}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.twist\_cmd}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{51}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{55}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.head\_cmd}
\NormalTok{obs[}\DecValTok{55}\NormalTok{:}\DecValTok{61}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.body\_cmd}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Basketball's command sampler sets the last ten values to zero. Both the
trainer and tests reject nonzero \texttt{51:61} values. Moving the ball
without changing robot proprioception leaves the observation unchanged
in the focused test. The actor is therefore \textbf{blind to explicit
ball state}: no ball position, velocity, rotation, radius, contact
label, or world position appears in the 61 values.

The word ``blind'' does not mean information-free. Contact with a moving
ball changes body angular velocity, projected gravity, joint state, and
the action-response history. The LSTM can use that time sequence to
infer useful hidden dynamics.

\subsubsection{Joint and action order}\label{joint-and-action-order}

The 14 action entries use this fixed servo order:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}rlr@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Action index & Joint & Default pose, rad \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
0 & left hip yaw & 0.0000 \\
1 & left hip roll & -0.0873 \\
2 & left hip pitch & -0.4579 \\
3 & left knee & -0.0049 \\
4 & left ankle & 0.4530 \\
5 & neck pitch & 0.3491 \\
6 & head pitch & 0.3491 \\
7 & head yaw & 0.0000 \\
8 & head roll & 0.0000 \\
9 & right hip yaw & 0.0000 \\
10 & right hip roll & 0.0873 \\
11 & right hip pitch & 0.4579 \\
12 & right knee & 0.0049 \\
13 & right ankle & -0.4530 \\
\end{longtable}
}

For raw actor output \(\mathbf a_t\in\mathbb R^{14}\), the applied
position target is

\[
\mathbf q^{\text{target}}_t
=\mathbf q^{\text{default}}
+\operatorname{clip}(\mathbf a_t,-4,4).
\]

The observation stores the \textbf{raw} action, not the clipped action.
This keeps the next observation and action-change penalty aware of what
the network requested. Under BAM actuation, the position target drives
the voltage-based servo model, including its inherited 3-6 physics-step
bus delay. Four physics steps make one control step, so that internal
delay is shorter than two control intervals.

\subsection{The recurrent actor and its state
lifecycle}\label{the-recurrent-actor-and-its-state-lifecycle}

\subsubsection{Network}\label{network}

The local actor is:

\[
61
\rightarrow \operatorname{LSTM}(256)
\rightarrow 512
\rightarrow 256
\rightarrow 128
\rightarrow 14,
\]

with ELU activations in the multilayer perceptron. The diagonal Gaussian
exploration scale has 14 trainable values and starts at the requested
\texttt{initial\_std}, usually \texttt{0.03}. It is a direct
standard-deviation parameter, clamped to at least \texttt{1e-6}, not a
log-standard-deviation parameter.

The observation normalizer computes

\[
\widehat{\mathbf o}
=\frac{\mathbf o-\boldsymbol\mu}
{\boldsymbol\sigma+0.01}.
\]

The imported source normalizer is frozen. Its stored standard deviation
for the ten zero-padded head/body slots is zero, so the \texttt{+0.01}
denominator keeps those divisions finite. The exported ONNX contains the
normalizer.

The local critic is different from the released upstream training critic
described in the source model card. The local
\texttt{Basketba\allowbreak{}llCritic\allowbreak{}} takes only the same
normalized 61 values and is a fresh feedforward
\texttt{61\ -\textgreater{}\ 512\ -\textgreater{}\ 256\ -\textgreater{}\ 128\ -\textgreater{}\ 1}
network. It has no ball-state input.

\subsubsection{One-step inference}\label{one-step-inference}

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}rlx/\allowbreak{}models/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/rlx/models/basketball.py}
\KeywordTok{def}\NormalTok{ forward(}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{, observations, h\_in, c\_in):}
\NormalTok{    normalized }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.obs\_normalizer(observations)}
\NormalTok{    recurrent, (h\_out, c\_out) }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.rnn.rnn(}
\NormalTok{        normalized.unsqueeze(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{),}
\NormalTok{        (h\_in, c\_in),}
\NormalTok{    )}
\NormalTok{    actions }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.mlp(recurrent.squeeze(}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{))}
    \ControlFlowTok{return}\NormalTok{ actions, h\_out, c\_out}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The ONNX interface is exact:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  inputs: \texttt{obs\ {[}1,61{]}}, \texttt{h\_in\ {[}1,1,256{]}},
  \texttt{c\_in\ {[}1,1,256{]}};
\item
  outputs: \texttt{actions\ {[}1,14{]}}, \texttt{h\_out\ {[}1,1,256{]}},
  \texttt{c\_out\ {[}1,1,256{]}};
\item
  data type: \texttt{float32}.
\end{itemize}

\subsubsection{When memory is carried and
reset}\label{when-memory-is-carried-and-reset}

At the beginning of a trial or episode, hidden state \(\mathbf h\) and
cell state \(\mathbf c\) are zero. At every ordinary 50 Hz step:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  send \texttt{obs}, \texttt{h\_in}, and \texttt{c\_in} to the actor;
\item
  apply \texttt{actions};
\item
  carry \texttt{h\_out} and \texttt{c\_out} into the next call.
\end{enumerate}

An ordinary command change does not reset memory. A trial reset, episode
boundary, policy activation/switch, or recovery boundary must reset both
tensors.

Training stores an \texttt{episode\_starts{[}time,\ env{]}} mask. During
collection and during every PPO replay, the code multiplies hidden and
cell state by zero where an episode starts:

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}rlx/\allowbreak{}models/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/rlx/models/basketball.py}
\ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ step }\KeywordTok{in} \BuiltInTok{range}\NormalTok{(observations.shape[}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{]):}
\NormalTok{    carry }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}\OperatorTok{\textasciitilde{}}\NormalTok{episode\_starts[step].}\BuiltInTok{bool}\NormalTok{()).to(observations.dtype)}
\NormalTok{    carry }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ carry.view(}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    hidden }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ hidden }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ carry}
\NormalTok{    cell }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ cell }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ carry}
\NormalTok{    actions, hidden, cell }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{(observations[step], hidden, cell)}
\NormalTok{    outputs.append(actions)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This matters because recurrent PPO cannot treat stored observations as
unrelated rows. It recomputes the full time sequence from the rollout's
initial hidden and cell tensors, applying the same episode masks. The
evaluator similarly calls \texttt{policy.reset()} once at each trial
start and carries state until first fall or the exact horizon.

The exporter runs a 40-step PyTorch/ONNX parity test with a state reset
at step 20. The retained fixed-command actor's maximum absolute action
error was \texttt{1.43e-6}; the mixed-command actor's was
\texttt{2.15e-6}.

\subsection{The exact local reward}\label{the-exact-local-reward}

\subsubsection{Symbols}\label{symbols}

Let:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathbf v_{xy}\) be body-frame forward and lateral trunk velocity;
\item
  \(\mathbf c_{xy}\) be the commanded forward and lateral velocity;
\item
  \(\boldsymbol\omega\) be trunk gyroscope values;
\item
  \(c_\omega\) be commanded yaw rate;
\item
  \(\mathbf g\) be gravity projected into the trunk frame;
\item
  \(\mathbf d_{xy}\) be trunk position minus ball position in the
  horizontal plane;
\item
  \(g_i\) be foot \(i\)'s radial gap from
  \texttt{BALL\_RADIUS\ +\ 0.012};
\item
  \(h\) be trunk height above the ball centre;
\item
  \(\mathbf v_b\) be horizontal ball velocity;
\item
  \(\mathbf a_t-\mathbf a_{t-1}\) be the raw action change.
\end{itemize}

Every weighted term is multiplied by \texttt{CTRL\_DT\ =\ 0.02}. The
reward is:

\[
r_t=0.02\sum_k w_k f_k.
\]

\subsubsection{Implemented formulas and
weights}\label{implemented-formulas-and-weights}

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3000}}
  >{\raggedleft\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.4000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Term
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Unweighted formula \(f_k\)
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedleft
Weight \(w_k\)
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
linear tracking & \(\exp(-\|\mathbf v_{xy}-\mathbf c_{xy}\|^2/0.1)\) &
1.0 \\
yaw tracking & \(\exp(-(\omega_z-c_\omega)^2/0.5)\) & 0.5 \\
upright & \(\exp(-\|(g_x,g_y)\|^2/0.09)\) & 1.0 \\
centred & \(\exp(-\|\mathbf d_{xy}\|^2/0.0016)\) & 2.0 \\
feet on ball & \(\frac12\sum_i\exp(-g_i^2/0.0004)\) & 1.0 \\
height & \(\exp(-(h-0.245)^2/0.0009)\) & 1.0 \\
ball-speed penalty & \(-\min(\|\mathbf v_b\|^2,100)\) & 0.05 \\
angular-velocity penalty & \(-\min(\omega_x^2+\omega_y^2,100)\) &
0.05 \\
action-rate penalty & \(-\min(\|\mathbf a_t-\mathbf a_{t-1}\|^2,100)\) &
0.2 \\
\end{longtable}
}

The source is compact enough to compare directly:

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}rlx/\allowbreak{}environm\allowbreak{}ents/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/rlx/environments/basketball.py}
\NormalTok{terms }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}
    \StringTok{"linear\_tracking"}\NormalTok{: math.exp(}
        \OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{((}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.body\_lin\_vel()[:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{{-}} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.twist\_cmd[:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{]) }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.1}
\NormalTok{    ),}
    \StringTok{"yaw\_tracking"}\NormalTok{: math.exp(}
        \OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{((}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.\_gyro[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{{-}} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.twist\_cmd[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{]) }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.5}
\NormalTok{    ),}
    \StringTok{"upright"}\NormalTok{: math.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(gravity[:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.09}\NormalTok{),}
    \StringTok{"centered"}\NormalTok{: math.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(relative[:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{)) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.0016}\NormalTok{),}
    \StringTok{"feet\_on\_ball"}\NormalTok{: }\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(np.mean(np.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{gaps }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2} \OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.0004}\NormalTok{))),}
    \StringTok{"height"}\NormalTok{: math.exp(}\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{((relative[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ BALL\_RADIUS }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{.125}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{/} \FloatTok{.0009}\NormalTok{),}
    \StringTok{"ball\_speed\_penalty"}\NormalTok{: }\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(ball\_velocity }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)),}
    \StringTok{"angular\_velocity\_penalty"}\NormalTok{: }\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(}\BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.\_gyro[:}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)),}
    \StringTok{"action\_rate\_penalty"}\NormalTok{: }\OperatorTok{{-}}\BuiltInTok{float}\NormalTok{(}
        \BuiltInTok{min}\NormalTok{(np.}\BuiltInTok{sum}\NormalTok{((}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.last\_action }\OperatorTok{{-}} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.prev\_action) }\OperatorTok{**} \DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{), }\DecValTok{100}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{    ),}
\NormalTok{\}}
\NormalTok{weighted }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ \{}
\NormalTok{    key: value }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ REWARD\_WEIGHTS[key] }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ CTRL\_DT}
    \ControlFlowTok{for}\NormalTok{ key, value }\KeywordTok{in}\NormalTok{ terms.items()}
\NormalTok{\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The reward uses privileged simulator state for the ball, contacts, and
geometry. That is allowed for training rewards, but none of it enters
the actor observation.

\subsubsection{Worked reward example}\label{worked-reward-example}

Suppose one step has:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  command \texttt{0.15\ m/s} forward and measured forward speed
  \texttt{0.01\ m/s};
\item
  no lateral error;
\item
  horizontal root-ball offset \texttt{0.02\ m};
\item
  reset-like height \texttt{h=0.247\ m};
\item
  raw action-change squared sum \texttt{0.04};
\item
  all other positive terms equal to 1 and other penalties zero.
\end{itemize}

Linear tracking:

\[
f_{\text{lin}}
=\exp\left(-\frac{(0.01-0.15)^2}{0.1}\right)
=\exp(-0.196)
\approx0.8220.
\]

Its weighted contribution is

\[
0.8220(1.0)(0.02)\approx0.01644.
\]

Centred:

\[
f_{\text{center}}
=\exp\left(-\frac{0.02^2}{0.0016}\right)
=e^{-0.25}
\approx0.7788,
\]

so the contribution is

\[
0.7788(2.0)(0.02)\approx0.03115.
\]

Height:

\[
f_{\text{height}}
=\exp\left(-\frac{(0.247-0.245)^2}{0.0009}\right)
\approx0.9956,
\]

giving approximately \texttt{0.01991}.

Action-rate penalty:

\[
(-0.04)(0.2)(0.02)=-0.00016.
\]

If every positive term were exactly 1 and every penalty were zero, the
maximum per-step total would be

\[
0.02(1+0.5+1+2+1+1)=0.13.
\]

This is a dense shaping score, not the success test. The evaluator
deliberately ignores reward totals.

\subsection{Physical-assistance
curriculum}\label{physical-assistance-curriculum}

\subsubsection{Ladder and transition
rule}\label{ladder-and-transition-rule}

The only curriculum levels are:

\textbf{Recorded text/data (not executable):} the implemented assistance
ladder.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{1.0 {-}\textgreater{} 0.5 {-}\textgreater{} 0.25 {-}\textgreater{} 0.1 {-}\textgreater{} 0.03 {-}\textgreater{} 0.0}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

After a completed episode:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  duration at least \texttt{6\ s}: move one level toward zero
  assistance;
\item
  duration below \texttt{1.5\ s}: move one level toward stronger
  assistance;
\item
  otherwise: remain at the same level.
\end{itemize}

The transition is applied on the next reset and only if the previous
episode actually finished. Reward weights never change.

\subsubsection{What assistance does}\label{what-assistance-does}

The assistance method first clears every external force. At
\texttt{hold=0}, it returns immediately. For nonzero hold, it applies:

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}rlx/\allowbreak{}environm\allowbreak{}ents/\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/rlx/environments/basketball.py}
\NormalTok{ball\_force }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{400} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (ball\_pos }\OperatorTok{{-}} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.\_ball\_anchor) }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{20} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ball\_vel[:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{ball\_force[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{20} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ball\_vel[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{]}
\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.xfrc\_applied[}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.ball\_body\_id, :}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.hold }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ ball\_force}

\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.xfrc\_applied[}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.ball\_body\_id, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{:] }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ (}
    \OperatorTok{{-}}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.hold }\OperatorTok{*} \FloatTok{.2} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (ball\_rotation }\OperatorTok{@}\NormalTok{ ball\_vel[}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{:])}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{duck\_force }\OperatorTok{=} \OperatorTok{{-}}\DecValTok{40} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}
    \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.xpos[}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.trunk\_body\_id] }\OperatorTok{{-}}\NormalTok{ ball\_pos}
\NormalTok{) }\OperatorTok{{-}} \DecValTok{4} \OperatorTok{*} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.qvel[:}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{]}
\NormalTok{duck\_force[}\DecValTok{2}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \DecValTok{0}
\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.xfrc\_applied[}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.trunk\_body\_id, :}\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.hold }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ duck\_force}

\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.data.xfrc\_applied[}\VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.trunk\_body\_id, }\DecValTok{3}\NormalTok{:] }\OperatorTok{=} \VariableTok{self}\NormalTok{.hold }\OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ (}
    \DecValTok{3} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ np.cross(up, [}\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{, }\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]) }\OperatorTok{{-}} \FloatTok{.08} \OperatorTok{*}\NormalTok{ angular\_world}
\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The ball receives horizontal spring-damper support toward its anchor,
vertical damping without a vertical position spring, and rotational
damping. The trunk receives horizontal centring force plus an
upright-restoring and angular-damping torque.

\textbf{Worked assistance example.} If the ball is displaced
\texttt{0.02\ m} in positive x with zero velocity, its unscaled x force
is

\[
-400(0.02)=-8\text{ N}.
\]

At \texttt{hold=0.25}, the applied x force is \texttt{-2\ N}. At
\texttt{hold=0}, it is exactly zero.

The retained curriculum smoke ran 7,168 environment steps with two
environments, completed 39 episodes, and exercised both promotion and
demotion. It ended with environments between \texttt{hold=0.25} and
\texttt{hold=0.5}. It did \textbf{not} graduate to zero and does not
demonstrate learning free-ball balance from scratch.

\subsection{Training: warm start, PPO, and the scratch
limit}\label{training-warm-start-ppo-and-the-scratch-limit}

\subsubsection{What is loaded}\label{what-is-loaded}

The direct trainer defaults to:

\textbf{Recorded text/data (not executable):} the default
source-checkpoint path.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{microduck{-}playground/artifacts/basketball/checkpoint.pt}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

It verifies the released source checkpoint and adjacent ONNX with fixed
SHA256 values. It then loads
\texttt{actor\_\allowbreak{}state\_\allowbreak{}dict}, freezes the
source observation normalizer, and overwrites the actor's exploration
standard deviation with
\texttt{-\allowbreak{}-\allowbreak{}initial-\allowbreak{}std}.

It does \textbf{not} restore the source critic, Adam optimizer, GPU
curriculum counter, or exact mjlab simulator state. Instead:

\textbf{Source excerpt (not standalone):}
\texttt{rlx/\allowbreak{}examples\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}ppo\_\allowbreak{}microduc\allowbreak{}k\_\allowbreak{}basketba\allowbreak{}ll.py}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\CommentTok{\# rlx/examples/ppo\_microduck\_basketball.py}
\NormalTok{actor, source\_metadata }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ load\_source\_actor(...)}
\NormalTok{critic }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ BasketballCritic(actor.obs\_normalizer)}
\NormalTok{optimizer }\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{ torch.optim.Adam(parameters, lr}\OperatorTok{=}\NormalTok{args.learning\_rate)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The retained summaries correctly label this:

\textbf{Recorded text/data (not executable):} retained run metadata.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{actor warm{-}start with fresh local critic and optimizer}
\NormalTok{full\_upstream\_resume: false}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

A local checkpoint may be used as another actor warm start, but it must
have the local format, frozen-normalization contract, an adjacent ONNX,
and recurrent parity. This is still not a full upstream resume.

\subsubsection{What ``scratch'' would
require}\label{what-scratch-would-require}

The local CLI has no \texttt{-\/-scratch} or random-actor option.
\texttt{train()} always calls \texttt{load\_source\_actor()}. The Studio
adapter also defaults to the released checkpoint or an
\texttt{-\/-init-from} checkpoint. Therefore the repository contains no
retained evidence that this local reward, curriculum, and CPU PPO
implementation can discover basketball balance from a random actor.

The mature b11 actor is the source of the demonstrated balance. Local
runs adapt it. The assistance smoke also begins from that actor. A truly
scratch experiment would need a deliberate code path, a declared random
initialization, a smoke test, and an unassisted first-fall evaluation.
None is claimed here.

\subsubsection{Rollout and PPO details}\label{rollout-and-ppo-details}

For \texttt{N} environments and \texttt{T} rollout steps, one update
collects

\[
N\times T
\]

transitions. The retained fixed-command run used \texttt{N=8},
\texttt{T=128}, and 100 updates:

\[
8\times128\times100=102{,}400
\]

new local transitions.

The actor samples

\[
\mathbf a_t
=\boldsymbol\mu_t+\boldsymbol\epsilon_t\odot\boldsymbol\sigma,
\qquad
\boldsymbol\epsilon_t\sim\mathcal N(\mathbf0,\mathbf I).
\]

GAE uses \texttt{gamma=0.99} and \texttt{lambda=0.95}. A true
termination does not bootstrap. A time-limit truncation uses the final
state's critic value in the one-step delta but stops the multi-step
trace across the reset. Advantages are normalized.

The PPO update uses one full recurrent sequence minibatch, five epochs,
clip coefficient \texttt{0.2}, value coefficient \texttt{1.0}, entropy
coefficient \texttt{0.01}, maximum gradient norm \texttt{1.0}, and
target KL \texttt{0.02}. It recomputes the complete LSTM sequence each
epoch. KL checking can stop later epochs, but a completed optimizer step
can still leave post-update KL above the target.

The fixed-command run used learning rate \texttt{2e-6}, initial standard
deviation \texttt{0.03}, and command \texttt{{[}0.15,0,0{]}}. The
mixed-command run separately used 153,600 steps, learning rate
\texttt{1e-5}, standard deviation \texttt{0.08}, and the direct
trainer's randomized sampler: exact zero 25\% of the time, otherwise
uniform forward \texttt{{[}-0.15,0.15{]}\ m/s}, lateral
\texttt{{[}-0.1,0.1{]}\ m/s}, and yaw \texttt{{[}-0.5,0.5{]}\ rad/s}.
They are independent warm starts, not a single 256,000-step lineage.

\begin{figure}
\centering
\includegraphics[width=6.2in,height=5.5in,alt={Observed PPO diagnostics for the two independent basketball continuation runs; the empty reward panel records that per-update reward history was not saved}]{assets/evidence/basketball-training-curves.png}
\caption{Observed PPO diagnostics for the two independent basketball
continuation runs; the empty reward panel records that per-update reward
history was not saved}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Parser-verified command line
workflow}\label{parser-verified-command-line-workflow}

All commands below run from the workspace root. Their option spellings
were checked against the current parsers. Training commands create new
artifacts and can take substantial time.

\subsubsection{Focused contract tests}\label{focused-contract-tests}

\textbf{Executable command (workspace root):}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\ExtensionTok{rlx/.venv{-}microduck/bin/python} \AttributeTok{{-}m}\NormalTok{ pytest }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{  rlx/tests/test\_basketball.py }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{  rlx/tests/test\_basketball\_policy.py }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{  rlx/tests/test\_basketball\_evaluation.py }\AttributeTok{{-}q}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\subsubsection{Direct five-update smoke}\label{direct-five-update-smoke}

Use a new output directory; the trainer refuses to overwrite a nonempty
run.

\textbf{Executable command (workspace root):}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\FunctionTok{bash}\NormalTok{ scripts/train{-}basketball.sh }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}
  \AttributeTok{{-}{-}output}\NormalTok{ rlx/artifacts/basketball{-}book{-}smoke }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}
  \AttributeTok{{-}{-}num{-}envs}\NormalTok{ 4 }\DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}

  \AttributeTok{{-}{-}no{-}curriculum} \DataTypeTok{\textbackslash{}}

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

This requests \texttt{4\ x\ 32\ x\ 5\ =\ 640} new transitions. It is a
wiring check, not learning evidence.

\subsection{Evaluation: balance is not
rolling}\label{evaluation-balance-is-not-rolling}

\subsubsection{First-fall accounting}\label{first-fall-accounting}

Evaluation does not auto-reset. It stops a trial at the first terminal
state or at the exact horizon. A complete trial requires:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  exactly the required number of samples;
\item
  no invalid physical metrics;
\item
  no termination or truncation;
\item
  zero automatic resets.
\end{itemize}

The environment terminates for low root height, root-ball offset above
\texttt{0.16\ m}, tilt above \texttt{55\ deg}, robot-floor contact,
non-foot robot-ball contact, or nonfinite physics. The evaluator is
stricter on accepted geometry: maximum tilt must remain below
\texttt{50\ deg}, and maximum root-ball offset below \texttt{0.16\ m}.

\subsubsection{Sustained balance}\label{sustained-balance}

A trial counts as sustained balance only when all are true:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\text{duration complete}\ \land\ \text{foot-contact fraction}\ge0.5\\
&\land\ \text{no body-ball contact}\ \land\ \text{no robot-floor contact}\\
&\land\ \text{hold always zero}\ \land\ \text{stable geometry}.
\end{aligned}
\]

Balance does not require the ball to remain at one world position. A
policy may balance while the ball drifts or turns.

\subsubsection{Controlled rolling}\label{controlled-rolling}

For a nonzero planar command, controlled rolling additionally requires:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  enough signed ball progress along the initial command direction;
\item
  low forward and lateral body-velocity error;
\item
  low yaw-rate error;
\item
  enough integrated ball rotation.
\end{enumerate}

For command speed \(s\), duration \(T\), and ball radius \(R=0.12\):

\[
d_{\min}=\max(0.25,\;0.35sT),
\]

\[
e_{\text{linear,max}}=\max(0.04,\;0.6s),
\]

\[
e_{\text{yaw,max}}=0.5,
\]

\[
\theta_{\min}
=\frac{\max(0.25,\;0.35sT)}{R}(0.5).
\]

\textbf{Worked 60-second gate.} With \texttt{s=0.15\ m/s}:

\[
sT=0.15(60)=9.0\text{ m},
\]

\[
d_{\min}=0.35(9.0)=3.15\text{ m},
\]

\[
e_{\text{linear,max}}=0.6(0.15)=0.09\text{ m/s},
\]

\[
\theta_{\min}=\frac{3.15}{0.12}(0.5)=13.125\text{ rad}.
\]

Ball travel alone is insufficient. The evaluator labels substantial
travel as random movement when signed command progress is too small
relative to the required distance or to total travel.

\subsubsection{Latest retained Studio
result}\label{latest-retained-studio-result}

The latest retained Studio evaluation uses BAM, action delay, random
initial yaw, no pushes, no observation noise, no mass/friction domain
randomization, no assistance, and no automatic reset.

\subsection{Reading artifacts without
overclaiming}\label{reading-artifacts-without-overclaiming}

Use the files for different questions:

{\def\LTcaptype{none} % do not increment counter
\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 2\tabcolsep) * \real{0.5000}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Artifact
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
What it can establish
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
\texttt{summary.json} & source hashes, settings, local steps, actor
change, parity, PPO diagnostics \\
\texttt{checkpoi\allowbreak{}nt.pt} & local actor, fresh local critic,
optimizer, and run metadata \\
\texttt{policy.onnx} & deterministic recurrent deployment graph with
frozen normalizer \\
\texttt{evaluati\allowbreak{}on.json} & every physical trial, criteria,
failures, and aggregate verdict \\
\texttt{rollout.mp4} & visible motion for one rendered seed and
command \\
\texttt{contact-\allowbreak{}sheet.pn\allowbreak{}g} & spaced visual
inspection across the rollout \\
Studio \texttt{result.json} & adapter-level pass/fail and provenance \\
\end{longtable}
}

Do not infer:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  steering from rotating ball texture;
\item
  straight commanded travel from total path length;
\item
  scratch learning from a curriculum smoke;
\item
  exact upstream continuation from actor warm-start training;
\item
  hardware safety from CPU MuJoCo;
\item
  bridge traversal from basketball balance.
\end{itemize}

The correct stopping statement for the retained policy is:

\begin{quote}
It sustains unassisted balance for all six retained 60-second Studio
trials. It fails all three commanded-forward rolling trials. Lateral and
yaw steering are unassessed. Bridge behavior and hardware behavior are
outside this evidence.
\end{quote}

\subsection{Exercises}\label{exercises-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  The ball radius is \texttt{0.12\ m}. What is the ball diameter?
\item
  At 50 Hz, how many control steps occur in 12 seconds?
\item
  Which observation indices hold the yaw command?
\item
  Does observation index 55 contain ball position?
\item
  A raw action at joint 4 is \texttt{0.20\ rad}. Ignoring clipping, what
  ankle target is applied when the default is \texttt{0.4530\ rad}?
\item
  Compute the centred reward's unweighted value for horizontal offset
  \texttt{0.04\ m}.
\item
  At \texttt{hold=0.5}, a stationary ball is displaced \texttt{0.01\ m}
  in x. What x assistance force is applied to the ball?
\item
  A curriculum environment at \texttt{hold=0.25} completes a
  \texttt{6.4\ s} episode. What is the next hold?
\item
  How many new transitions are collected by 8 environments, 128 steps,
  and 25 updates?
\item
  For \texttt{0.15\ m/s} over 60 seconds, why is \texttt{1.30\ m} signed
  progress a failure even if the robot remains upright?
\item
  A trial has 60-second duration, 90\% foot contact, zero hold, no
  forbidden contacts, \texttt{4\ deg} maximum tilt, and \texttt{0.02\ m}
  maximum offset. The ball travels \texttt{4\ m} sideways under a
  forward command. Does it pass sustained balance? Does it pass
  controlled rolling?
\item
  Why is the retained 7,168-step curriculum smoke not evidence of
  scratch learning?
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Answers}\label{answers-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \(2R=2(0.12)=0.24\text{ m}\).
\item
  \(12/0.02=600\) steps.
\item
  Index 50, the third value in slice \texttt{48:51}.
\item
  No.~Slice \texttt{55:61} is zero body-command padding. The actor
  receives no explicit ball state.
\item
  \(0.4530+0.20=0.6530\text{ rad}\).
\item
  \(\exp(-0.04^2/0.0016)=\exp(-1)\approx0.3679\).
\item
  Unscaled force is \(-400(0.01)=-4\text{ N}\); half hold applies
  \texttt{-2\ N}.
\item
  The ladder moves one step toward zero:
  \texttt{0.25\ -\textgreater{}\ 0.1}.
\item
  \(8\times128\times25=25{,}600\) transitions.
\item
  The gate requires at least \texttt{3.15\ m} signed progress, so
  \texttt{1.30\ m} is below target. Upright survival alone is balance,
  not controlled rolling.
\item
  It passes sustained balance because those conditions satisfy the
  balance gate. It fails controlled rolling because sideways travel does
  not provide enough signed forward progress and likely fails velocity
  tracking.
\item
  The smoke started from the mature released actor, used physical
  assistance, and ended above zero assistance. The local CLI did not
  initialize a random actor, and no unassisted scratch policy was
  evaluated.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Suspended Bridge: Learning to Walk on a Moving
Support}\label{suspended-bridge-learning-to-walk-on-a-moving-support}

\subsection{Learning objectives}\label{learning-objectives-1}

After this chapter, you should be able to:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  describe the simulated bridge geometry, four tendon cables, free
  plank, platforms, and non-colliding gantry with exact dimensions;
\item
  distinguish a simulated bill of materials from a certified physical
  construction plan;
\item
  trace the unchanged 61-observation and 14-action deployment contract;
\item
  explain why the navigation commander may use world state while the
  learned actor receives only the standard command slots;
\item
  derive every inherited locomotion reward term and its actual weight;
\item
  explain why the curriculum changes physics and spawn position, but
  never reward weights;
\item
  describe the walking-policy transfer: transferred actor and
  normalizer, frozen normalization statistics, fresh critic, and fresh
  optimizer;
\item
  reproduce the current build, train, export, evaluation, and render
  commands; and
\item
  apply the strict full-20-second crossing gate without turning partial
  progress into a successful result.
\end{enumerate}

The most important result comes first:

\begin{quote}
The recorded \texttt{bridge-\allowbreak{}studio-\allowbreak{}02} pilot
trained for 32,768 PPO transitions and did \textbf{not} cross the
suspended bridge. Its best measured progress was 1.175 m from the launch
position, below the 1.60 m progress threshold and without a
contact-validated two-foot landing. This chapter documents a working
simulation and training protocol, not a mastered skill.
\end{quote}

\subsection{Evidence and claim
boundary}\label{evidence-and-claim-boundary-1}

This chapter is grounded in the current repository implementation:

The local training playbook adds three interpretation rules:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  never change the 61/14 interface for one task;
\item
  do not treat reward or PPO completion as proof of behavior; and
\item
  inspect a deterministic exported rollout before claiming a skill.
\end{itemize}

The bridge model is a \textbf{local MuJoCo prototype}. Its dimensions,
masses, friction coefficients, tendon stiffness, damping, and limits are
simulator parameters. They are not measurements from a constructed
bridge, cable ratings, load calculations, a safety factor, or a hardware
certification. The render material names such as
\texttt{bridge\_\allowbreak{}plank\_\allowbreak{}mat} describe color and
appearance, not a specified species of wood or grade of metal.

\subsection{What is physically
simulated?}\label{what-is-physically-simulated}

The task places Microduck on a raised launch platform. A narrow plank
spans the gap to a second platform. The plank is not welded, hinged, or
kinematically animated. It has a MuJoCo free joint, so contact forces
can translate it and rotate it in roll, pitch, and yaw. Four spatial
tendons connect moving attachment sites on the plank to fixed world
anchors.

\subsubsection{Simulated bill of
materials}\label{simulated-bill-of-materials}

This table is a simulator inventory, not a shopping or construction
list.
